<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which option best describes the biological function of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> A conventional Rho-family GTPase that cycles between GDP- and GTP-bound states and promotes actomyosin stress-fiber formation.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> A Ras-family GTPase that stimulates mitogenic signaling by alternating between inactive GDP-bound and active GTP-bound states.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> An atypical Rho-family GTPase with low affinity for GDP and no intrinsic GTPase activity; it remains GTP-bound and promotes actin rearrangement, including Rac-dependent neurite formation through disruption of cortical actin.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> A Rab-family GTPase that regulates vesicle transport by cycling between GDP- and GTP-bound states.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br>The protein sequence begins with &quot;MKERRAPQPVVARCKLVLVGDVQCGKTAMLQVL...&quot; which is a direct match to human Rnd1 (also known as RhoL). Let me analyze the key features:<br><br>- **P-loop motif**: &quot;GDVQCGKT&quot; — this is a Rho-family P-loop signature (GXXXXGKT), distinguishing it from Ras-family (which has &quot;GDGQVGKGKT&quot;) and Rab-family proteins.<br>- **Atypical Rho GTPase characteristics**: Rnd1 belongs to the Rnd/RhoL subfamily of atypical Rho GTPases. Unlike conventional Rho GTPases (e.g., RhoA, Rac1), Rnd1:<br>  - Lacks detectable intrinsic GTPase activity (does not efficiently hydrolyze GTP)<br>  - Has very low affinity for GDP<br>  - Is constitutively maintained in the GTP-bound state<br>  - Does not require GEFs for activation<br>- **Biological function**: Rnd1 promotes actin cytoskeleton rearrangement and has been specifically shown to promote Rac-dependent neurite formation through disruption of cortical actin networks.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- A: Describes conventional Rho GTPases like RhoA — Rnd1 is NOT conventional (lacks GTPase activity, constitutively active).<br>- B: Ras-family — sequence does not match Ras P-loop signature.<br>- C: Perfectly matches Rnd1&#x27;s atypical properties (no intrinsic GTPase activity, low GDP affinity, constitutively GTP-bound, promotes actin rearrangement/neurite formation via cortical actin disruption).<br>- D: Rab-family — sequence does not match Rab family signatures.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Considering the sequence and the possibility that different small-GTPase families share nucleotide-binding features, which functional model best fits this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> A conventional Rho-family switch that cycles between GDP- and GTP-bound states and promotes actomyosin stress-fiber formation.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> A Ras-family switch that cycles between GDP- and GTP-bound states to stimulate mitogenic signaling.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> An atypical Rho-family protein with low GDP affinity and no intrinsic GTPase activity, which remains GTP-bound and promotes actin rearrangement, including Rac-dependent neuritic process formation partly through disruption of cortical actin filaments.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> A Rab-family switch that cycles between GDP- and GTP-bound states to regulate vesicle transport.<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_molecular_function_0005:1<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Sequence Analysis:**<br>The protein sequence (~190 amino acids) contains the characteristic small GTPase G-domain with the five conserved G-box motifs:<br>- G1 (P-loop): &quot;GDVQCGKT&quot; (Walker A motif)<br>- G2 (Switch II): &quot;SDSD&quot; region<br>- G3 (Switch I): Conserved aspartate residues<br>- G4: &quot;CKTD&quot; region<br>- G5: &quot;NKPSPLP&quot; region<br><br>The C-terminus ends with &quot;KSCSIM&quot; containing a &quot;C&quot; that could serve as a prenylation site (CAAX-like motif), consistent with membrane-associated Rho proteins.<br><br>**Identification:**<br>This sequence corresponds to **TC10 (RhoQ/ARHG21)**, an atypical member of the Rho GTPase family. Key evidence:<br>- N-terminal sequence &quot;MKERRAPQPVVARC&quot; is characteristic of TC10<br>- The overall length and G-domain structure match Rho family but with atypical features<br>- The C-terminal &quot;CSIM&quot; serves as a palmitoylation/prenylation motif<br><br>**Functional Evaluation:**<br>- **Option A (Conventional Rho):** Incorrect. TC10 does not function as a typical Rho with efficient GDP/GTP cycling promoting stress fibers.<br>- **Option B (Ras-family):** Incorrect. Rab proteins have different structural features and function in vesicular trafficking.<br><br>TC10&#x27;s unique biology as an atypical Rho GTPase that is essentially &quot;locked&quot; in the GTP-bound state and mediates neurite outgrowth through actin remodeling makes Option C the best functional model.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>

The Rieske [2Fe-2S] cluster is the only type of iron-sulfur cluster that uses histidines as ligands (C-x3-H pattern), which distinguishes it from both [4Fe-4S] clusters (all Cys) and plant-type [2Fe-2S] clusters (all Cys).

- The Fe atoms are each coordinated by one Cys and one His
- The sequence motif is typically written as C-x3-H or sometimes C-x2-H depending on the protein family
